			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Bring structured and unstructured data together with Amazon Quick</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/bring-structured-and-unstructured-data-together-with-amazon-quick/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Puneeth Komaragiri]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 03 Nov 2026 16:49:29 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Quick Suite]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">a3db7b2c3fdf8a48e563f027e982fa25a50c5a02</guid>

					<description>Amazon Quick brings structured and unstructured data together in one experience. In this post, you connect Amazon S3 as a knowledge base, enrich a dataset, build a Space, and create a custom chat agent that answers business questions across both data types through a single conversational interface.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Organizations collect data in many forms. Structured data lives in databases and spreadsheets, whereas unstructured data sits in documents, reports, and files stored across file systems. Analyzing both together typically requires switching between multiple tools, running separate queries, and manually correlating results.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/welcome.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick&lt;/a&gt; addresses this with a unified experience. You can connect structured data through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/working-with-datasets.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;enriched datasets&lt;/a&gt; and unstructured data through &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/s3-integration.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;S3-backed knowledge bases&lt;/a&gt;. You can then query both through a single conversational AI agent.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, you learn how to:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Connect Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) as a data source and create a knowledge base.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Build a Space that combines structured and unstructured data.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Create and configure a custom chat agent that queries across both data types.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Ask questions that span structured and unstructured sources in a single conversation.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-architecture"&gt;Solution architecture&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram illustrates how each component connects to deliver a unified data experience through Amazon Quick.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-1.png" alt="Architecture diagram showing the data, workspace, and agent layers that connect structured datasets and an Amazon S3 knowledge base to a chat agent in Amazon Quick" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Solution architecture for combining structured and unstructured data in Amazon Quick&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The architecture has three layers:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Data layer:&lt;/strong&gt; Your data exists in two forms. Unstructured content (documents, PDFs, reports) is stored in Amazon S3 and indexed through a knowledge base. Structured data (metrics, sales figures, and operational key performance indicators) is loaded as enriched datasets with semantic descriptions that the agent uses to translate questions into SQL.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Workspace layer:&lt;/strong&gt; A Space brings both data types together into a single workspace. The Space references your knowledge base for unstructured queries and your datasets for structured queries. It can also include dashboards, file uploads, and action connectors. This is the layer where data from different sources converges into a unified context.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Agent layer:&lt;/strong&gt; The custom chat agent links to the Space and uses it as its knowledge source. When a user asks a question, the agent determines whether to query the knowledge base, the dataset, or both, and returns a combined response with citations. Amazon Quick also applies built-in &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/guardrails.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AI guardrails&lt;/a&gt;, such as content safety screening, to chat interactions.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With this layered approach, you can expand the solution over time. When you add a new data source to the Space, the agent linked to that Space can use it, and responses respect the permissions of the user who asks.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To follow along with this walkthrough, you need:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An Amazon Quick account with an Enterprise subscription for the user who follows this walkthrough (creating datasets requires Enterprise). For details, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick pricing&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An Amazon S3 bucket for your business documents. To create one, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/create-bucket-overview.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon S3 User Guide&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/s3-admin-setup.html#s3-grant-bucket-access" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick access to your S3 bucket&lt;/a&gt;, granted by an Amazon Quick administrator. In the admin console, the administrator chooses Permissions, then AWS resources, selects Amazon S3, and chooses Select S3 buckets. Selected buckets have read-only permissions by default.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Permissions to create integrations, knowledge bases, datasets, spaces, and chat agents in Amazon Quick.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prepare-your-sample-data"&gt;Prepare your sample data&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This walkthrough uses two types of data:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Unstructured documents: &lt;/strong&gt;Upload these to your Amazon S3 bucket for the knowledge base:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws-blogs-artifacts-public.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/BI-7992/AnyCompany_Business_Review.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AnyCompany_Business_Review.pdf&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws-blogs-artifacts-public.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/BI-7992/AnyCompany_Market_Analysis_Report.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AnyCompany_Market_Analysis_Report.pdf&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws-blogs-artifacts-public.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/BI-7992/AnyCompany_Product_Strategy_Memo.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AnyCompany_Product_Strategy_Memo.pdf&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Structured data: &lt;/strong&gt;Download this file to your computer. You upload it directly to Amazon Quick in Step 3:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws-blogs-artifacts-public.s3.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/BI-7992/Product_Sales.csv" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Product_Sales.csv&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-1-create-an-amazon-s3-integration"&gt;Step 1: Create an Amazon S3 integration&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Start by connecting Amazon Quick to your S3 bucket. From the Amazon Quick console, select &lt;strong&gt;Knowledge&lt;/strong&gt; in the left navigation panel.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The knowledge page shows available connector types, including Amazon Q Business, Web Crawler, Amazon S3, and third-party options like Google Drive, OneDrive, and Microsoft SharePoint.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Under Amazon S3, choose &lt;strong&gt;Add&lt;/strong&gt;. In the Create S3 knowledge base dialog, on the Connect S3 bucket page, provide the following details:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Name:&lt;/strong&gt; A descriptive name for your integration (for example, “My Data Access Integration”).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;S3 bucket location:&lt;/strong&gt; Choose &lt;strong&gt;Quick Suite instance account&lt;/strong&gt; to use a bucket in the same AWS account as Amazon Quick, or &lt;strong&gt;Other AWS account&lt;/strong&gt; for a bucket in a different account.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;S3 bucket URL:&lt;/strong&gt; The S3 URI of the bucket containing your data. The bucket must be in the same Region as Amazon Quick.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Next&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-2.png" alt="Create S3 knowledge base dialog showing the Connect S3 bucket fields for name, bucket location, and bucket URL" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Create integration dialog showing the S3 connection fields&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-2-create-a-knowledge-base"&gt;Step 2: Create a knowledge base&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After the S3 integration is created, Amazon Quick prompts you to create a knowledge base. The knowledge base indexes your S3 content for AI-powered search.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Configure the knowledge base:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Name:&lt;/strong&gt; “Business Knowledge Base”.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Description (optional):&lt;/strong&gt; Notes about how the knowledge base will be used.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Content:&lt;/strong&gt; Select “Add all content” to index everything in the connected S3 bucket, or “Add specific content” to specify the S3 prefixes for the folders and files to include (prefix filters are case-sensitive).&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Next:&lt;/strong&gt; Additional settings opens optional settings for document-level access control lists (ACLs) and a metadata files folder. Decide whether to use ACLs before you create the knowledge base, because you can’t change that choice later. This walkthrough uses sample data, so the default settings are sufficient. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/s3-setup-manage.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Set up and manage Amazon S3 integration&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Create&lt;/strong&gt;. A success banner confirms the S3 integration, and the knowledge base begins indexing in the background.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-3.png" alt="Create knowledge base dialog with the Business Knowledge Base name and content selection options" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: Create knowledge base dialog with the Business Knowledge Base name and content options&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-3-create-and-enrich-a-dataset"&gt;Step 3: Create and enrich a dataset&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/working-with-datasets.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;dataset&lt;/a&gt; is your structured data file loaded into Amazon Quick. By enriching the dataset with semantic descriptions, custom instructions, and correct data types, the AI agent can interpret your data more accurately. It can then translate natural language questions into SQL queries.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="create-the-dataset"&gt;Create the dataset&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;From the Amazon Quick home page, navigate to &lt;strong&gt;Data&lt;/strong&gt; in the left navigation.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Create dataset&lt;/strong&gt;, then select &lt;strong&gt;Upload file&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Select the &lt;strong&gt;Product_Sales.csv&lt;/strong&gt; file you downloaded earlier and choose &lt;strong&gt;Open&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;On the confirmation screen, review the column names and data types. Choose &lt;strong&gt;Next&lt;/strong&gt; to create the dataset.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick ingests the file into SPICE (the in-memory calculation engine) and the dataset appears in your dataset list.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The dataset takes its name from the uploaded file, so it appears as Product_Sales.csv in your account.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="enrich-the-dataset"&gt;Enrich the dataset&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Enriching your dataset with semantic information helps the AI understand your data in business context. The agent uses this context to translate natural language questions into more accurate SQL queries. From the dataset page, choose &lt;strong&gt;Edit dataset&lt;/strong&gt; to enter the data preparation view.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="transform-fix-data-types"&gt;Transform: Fix data types&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In the &lt;strong&gt;Transform&lt;/strong&gt; tab, update data types for columns that were auto-detected incorrectly. For this dataset:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Change &lt;strong&gt;Order Date&lt;/strong&gt; and &lt;strong&gt;Date Key&lt;/strong&gt; from String to Date.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Change &lt;strong&gt;Customer ID&lt;/strong&gt; from String to Integer.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Change &lt;strong&gt;Sales&lt;/strong&gt;, &lt;strong&gt;Discount&lt;/strong&gt;, and &lt;strong&gt;Profit&lt;/strong&gt; from String to Decimal.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Change &lt;strong&gt;Quantity&lt;/strong&gt; and &lt;strong&gt;Unit Price&lt;/strong&gt; from String to Integer.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-4.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-4.png" alt="Transform tab showing data type changes applied to columns in the Product_Sales.csv dataset" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: Transform view showing data type changes applied to the Product_Sales.csv dataset&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="output-add-semantic-context"&gt;Output: Add semantic context&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Select the &lt;strong&gt;Output&lt;/strong&gt; tab to access the dataset-level enrichment fields. Here you provide context that helps the AI understand what your data represents and how to use it.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Description:&lt;/strong&gt; Add a dataset-level description:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;“Product sales transaction data for AnyCompany, covering orders from FY2024-FY2025 across North America, EMEA, Asia Pacific, and Latin America. Includes order details, customer information, product lines, and revenue metrics.”&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Custom instructions:&lt;/strong&gt; Add guidance for how the AI should query this data:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;USAGE GUIDELINES:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Revenue values are in USD.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;When comparing performance, group by Region (North America, EMEA, Asia Pacific, Latin America).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Order Date should be used for time-based trend analysis. The fiscal year matches the calendar year.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Date Key is a numeric YYYYMMDD representation of Order Date.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Sales = Quantity x Unit Price x (1 – Discount)&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;COMMON USE CASES:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Regional sales performance comparison.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Product line revenue analysis.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Quarterly trend identification.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Customer segmentation by industry and segment.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;TAGS:&lt;/strong&gt; Sales, revenue, orders, regional performance, product analytics&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Column descriptions:&lt;/strong&gt; Select each column in the left panel and add a description. For example:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Order ID: “Unique identifier for each sales transaction (format: &lt;code&gt;REGION-YYYY-NNNNNN&lt;/code&gt;)”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Region: “Geographic sales region: North America, EMEA, Asia Pacific, or Latin America”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Product: “AnyCompany product name: Analytics Pro, Connect, Automate, AI Assistant, Data Catalog, or Integration Hub”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Sales: “Order revenue in USD after discount”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Segment: “Customer size segment: Enterprise, Mid-Market, or SMB”&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-5.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-5.png" alt="Output tab showing the dataset Description and Custom instructions fields filled in" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: Output view showing dataset details with Description and Custom instructions configured&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Save &amp;amp; publish&lt;/strong&gt; to save your changes. The dataset summary now shows the columns with their correct data types and the descriptions you added.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-6.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-6.png" alt="Enriched Product_Sales.csv dataset summary listing 16 columns with their data types and descriptions" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 6: Enriched Product_Sales.csv dataset summary showing all 16 columns, data types, and descriptions&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This enriched dataset provides the structured data layer for your solution. When the agent receives a natural language question about sales data, it uses these descriptions and instructions to generate more accurate SQL queries. Combined with the knowledge base from Step 2, you now have both structured and unstructured data ready to bring together in a Space.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-4-create-a-space"&gt;Step 4: Create a Space&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/working-with-spaces.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Spaces&lt;/a&gt; in Amazon Quick act as unified data workspaces. A Space can hold file uploads, dashboards, knowledge bases, actions, and datasets, giving your chat agent a single location to query across data types.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;From the left navigation, select &lt;strong&gt;Spaces&lt;/strong&gt; and create a new Space. Name it “Business Intelligence Hub”.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Add your knowledge base and dataset to the Space:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Add knowledge&lt;/strong&gt; and select &lt;strong&gt;Knowledge bases&lt;/strong&gt; from the list. Select “Business Knowledge Base” from the list.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Add knowledge&lt;/strong&gt; again and select &lt;strong&gt;Datasets&lt;/strong&gt; from the list. Select “Product_Sales.csv” from the list.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Space now shows both your knowledge base (unstructured documents) and your enriched dataset (structured sales data) linked as data sources. This is the central hub where both data types come together, giving your chat agent a single place to query across everything. Figure 7 shows the Space with both resources in the Ready state.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-7.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-7.png" alt="Business Intelligence Hub Space showing the Product_Sales.csv dataset and Business Knowledge Base linked as data sources in the Ready state" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 7: Business Intelligence Hub Space showing the Product_Sales.csv dataset and Business Knowledge Base&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-5-create-a-custom-chat-agent"&gt;Step 5: Create a custom chat agent&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Navigate to &lt;strong&gt;Chat agents&lt;/strong&gt; in the left navigation and choose &lt;strong&gt;Create chat agent&lt;/strong&gt;. Amazon Quick presents a prompt builder where you can describe your agent in natural language or choose from templates like Training Guide, Project Coordinator, and Onboarding Helper.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For this walkthrough, choose &lt;strong&gt;Skip&lt;/strong&gt; to configure the agent manually.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 702px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-8.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-8.png" alt="Chat agent creation screen with a prompt builder and sample agent templates" width="692"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 8: Chat agent creation screen with the prompt builder and sample templates&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="configure-the-agent-persona"&gt;Configure the agent persona&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The configuration panel has several sections. Start with the Agent Persona:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Agent identity:&lt;/strong&gt; “Business Intelligence Assistant”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Description:&lt;/strong&gt; “An AI assistant that breaks down information silos by analyzing business data from your knowledge base”&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For Agent Persona instructions, enter:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-plaintext"&gt;You are a Business Intelligence Assistant designed to help organizations break down information silos. Your primary functions are:

1. Analyze business data stored in S3
2. Retrieve relevant findings from the reports and memos in the knowledge base
3. Provide comprehensive insights that connect internal data with external trends
4. Generate clear, actionable recommendations based on data analysis

When answering questions, always cite your sources and explain how you arrived at your conclusions. Focus on delivering practical business value and highlighting patterns across different information sources.&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A note appears confirming that your chat agent comes equipped with enterprise guardrails.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-9.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-9.png" alt="Agent persona configuration with the agent identity and persona instructions filled in" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 9: Agent persona configuration with identity and persona instructions filled in&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="set-communication-style"&gt;Set communication style&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Under Communication style, pick a response style preset. Options include Executive, Technical, and Creative. For a business intelligence use case, select &lt;strong&gt;Executive&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="link-knowledge-sources"&gt;Link knowledge sources&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Under &lt;strong&gt;Knowledge Sources&lt;/strong&gt;, choose &lt;strong&gt;Link spaces&lt;/strong&gt; and select the “Business Intelligence Hub” Space you created earlier. This gives the agent access to your S3 knowledge base and other data sources in that Space. When you link a Space, the agent answers from large language model (LLM) knowledge and the linked resources only.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You can also attach reference documents (PDF, TXT, HTML, MD, CSV, DOC, DOCX) directly to the agent for additional context. Amazon Quick extracts up to 100,000 characters of text from the reference documents that you upload.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-10.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-10.png" alt="Knowledge Sources section showing the Business Intelligence Hub Space linked to the agent" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 10: Knowledge Sources section showing the linked Business Intelligence Hub Space&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="customize-the-agent"&gt;Customize the agent&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Under Customization, configure:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Welcome message:&lt;/strong&gt; “Hello! I’m your Business Intelligence Assistant. I can help you analyze your sales data and business documents and provide insights by comparing internal information with market trends. How can I help you today?”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Suggested prompts:&lt;/strong&gt; “Summarize key insights from our reports” and “Analyze our quarterly sales performance”&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-11.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-11.png" alt="Customization section showing the welcome message and suggested prompts for the agent" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 11: Customization section with the welcome message and suggested prompts&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="launch-the-agent"&gt;Launch the agent&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Launch chat agent&lt;/strong&gt;. The right panel shows a live preview of your agent with the welcome message and suggested prompts displayed.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-12.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-12.png" alt="Launched Business Intelligence Assistant showing the welcome message and suggested prompts in the chat panel" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 12: Launched Business Intelligence Assistant with the welcome message and suggested prompts in the chat panel&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-6-query-across-structured-and-unstructured-data"&gt;Step 6: Query across structured and unstructured data&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With the agent running, start with a question about your business results:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;How did sales change by region in 2025?&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For this question, the agent combines results from the Product_Sales.csv dataset with the documents in the S3 knowledge base. It returns a response with regional sales breakdowns, trends, and citations that point back to each source.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Figure 13 shows the response. Each citation marker links a figure back to its source document.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-13.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-13.png" alt="Chat agent response with a regional sales analysis drawn from the dataset and the knowledge base documents, including source citations" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 13: Chat agent response showing regional sales analysis from the dataset and the knowledge base documents&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you ask a question about structured data, the agent uses text-to-SQL to translate your natural language into a SQL query. It runs the query against the dataset and returns the results in a readable format. This means you can ask complex analytical questions without writing SQL yourself.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Next, ask about a specific document:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;What are the key strategic priorities outlined in our product strategy memo?&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent summarizes the strategic pillars, the FY2026 roadmap, and the investment requirements from the Product Strategy Memo, as shown in Figure 14.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-14.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-14.png" alt="Chat agent response summarizing the strategic priorities from the Product Strategy Memo" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 14: Chat agent response summarizing strategic priorities from the Product Strategy Memo&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Finally, ask a question that needs both data types:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;How do our 2025 sales results align with the market trends identified in our analysis report?&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent compares the trends in the Market Analysis Report with results from your sales data and cites each source, as shown in Figure 15.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-15.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-15.png" alt="Chat agent response that compares 2025 sales results against market trends cited from the Market Analysis Report" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 15: Chat agent response comparing 2025 sales results with market trends from the Market Analysis Report&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This is the core value of combining data types in a single Space. The agent can pull from structured CSV data and unstructured documents in the same response. You don’t need to know where the data lives.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-7-generate-artifacts-and-reports-using-natural-language"&gt;Step 7: Generate artifacts and reports using natural language&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The Business Intelligence Assistant can also generate formatted documents, including Microsoft PowerPoint presentations, Microsoft Excel spreadsheets, and Microsoft Word documents. Try these prompts in the chat agent:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;“Create a PowerPoint presentation summarizing AnyCompany’s Q4 2025 sales performance by region, including key trends and recommendations.”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;“Generate an Excel spreadsheet showing the top 10 orders by revenue with Order ID, Region, Product, and Revenue columns.”&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;“Create a Word document with a quarterly business review summary based on our annual review data.”&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-16.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-16.png" alt="Chat agent confirming the request to create a Q4 2025 sales PowerPoint and preparing a presentation outline" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 16: Chat agent confirming the request and preparing a PowerPoint outline for AnyCompany’s Q4 2025 sales performance&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-17.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/27/ML-22035-17.png" alt="Chat agent showing the generated eight-slide PowerPoint and a downloadable pptx file" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 17: Generated eight-slide PowerPoint with a downloadable AnyCompany_FY2025_Sales_Performance.pptx file&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-8-share-the-agent"&gt;Step 8: Share the agent&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When you launch a chat agent, it remains private until you share it. After you test the agent, share it with your team:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;From the left navigation, choose &lt;strong&gt;Chat agents&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the &lt;strong&gt;Actions&lt;/strong&gt; column for Business Intelligence Assistant, choose the menu icon, and then choose &lt;strong&gt;Share&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Enter the users or groups to share with, assign &lt;strong&gt;Owner&lt;/strong&gt; or &lt;strong&gt;Viewer&lt;/strong&gt; permissions, and then choose &lt;strong&gt;Share&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent returns permissions-aware responses, and a knowledge base is available only to its creator until you update its permissions. Make sure that the people you share the agent with also have access to the Space, the knowledge base, and the dataset. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/custom-agents.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Custom chat agents&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="what-you-accomplished"&gt;What you accomplished&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this walkthrough, you:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Connected Amazon S3 as a data source through an integration.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Created a knowledge base that indexes unstructured business documents.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Created and enriched a Product Sales dataset for structured data queries.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Built a Space that serves as a unified data hub for your chat agent.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Configured a custom chat agent with a defined persona and linked knowledge sources.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Queried the agent across structured and unstructured data in a single conversation.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Generated a PowerPoint presentation from a chat prompt.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Shared the agent with your team.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="best-practices"&gt;Best practices&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Use the following practices as you move from this walkthrough to your own data:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Organize your S3 data:&lt;/strong&gt; Use clear folder structures and file naming conventions. A clear structure also makes it more straightforward to scope a knowledge base to specific S3 prefixes.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Write specific persona instructions:&lt;/strong&gt; The more specific your instructions, the more consistent the agent responses. Include guidance on how to cite sources and handle ambiguous queries.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use Spaces to control scope:&lt;/strong&gt; Rather than giving an agent access to everything, create focused Spaces with only the data sources relevant to that agent’s purpose.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Start with suggested prompts:&lt;/strong&gt; Suggested prompts help users understand what the agent can do. Choose prompts that demonstrate the agent’s range across both structured and unstructured data.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Test before sharing:&lt;/strong&gt; Use the preview panel to test your agent with representative questions before sharing it with your team.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="security-considerations"&gt;Security considerations&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Under the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/compliance/shared-responsibility-model/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS shared responsibility model&lt;/a&gt;, you are responsible for configuring access to your data, knowledge bases, and agents. Consider the following as you adapt this solution:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Grant least-privilege S3 access:&lt;/strong&gt; Give Amazon Quick access only to the buckets that your knowledge bases need. Buckets that an administrator selects have read-only permissions by default. If you create knowledge bases through the API, you can provide a custom AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) role for an individual S3 data source instead of granting account-wide S3 access.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Control document access:&lt;/strong&gt; If documents require per-user or per-group access, enable document-level ACLs when you create the knowledge base. You can’t change this choice later.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Encrypted buckets:&lt;/strong&gt; If your bucket uses AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS) keys, follow the AWS KMS steps in the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/s3-admin-setup.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;administrator setup guide&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Add only trusted content:&lt;/strong&gt; The agent uses the documents in your knowledge base to form responses, so include only content from sources you trust.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Share selectively:&lt;/strong&gt; Chat agents and knowledge bases are private by default. Share them only with the users and groups that need them.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use guardrails as one layer:&lt;/strong&gt; Amazon Quick includes built-in content safety screening, and administrators can add up to 50 blocked words or phrases. These controls help reduce risk but don’t replace your responsibility to configure access and permissions. For details, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quick/latest/userguide/guardrails.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AI guardrails in Amazon Quick&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, remove the resources you created:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the chat agent from the Chat agents page.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the Space from the Spaces page.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the Product_Sales.csv dataset from the Datasets page.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the knowledge base and S3 integration from the Knowledge page.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;If you created an S3 bucket specifically for this walkthrough, empty and delete it. Deleting a bucket and its objects is permanent, so back up any data that you want to keep.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick user subscriptions and account fees are billed separately from these resources. For details, see &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/quick/pricing/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Quick pricing&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Quick helps you bring structured and unstructured data together. In this post, you connected S3 as a knowledge base, enriched a dataset, built a Space, and created a custom chat agent. Business users can now query both data types through a single conversational interface, without needing to know where the data lives or how to write queries.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p style="clear: both"&gt; &lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>ServiceNow on-behalf-of token exchange with Bedrock AgentCore Gateway</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/servicenow-on-behalf-of-token-exchange-with-bedrock-agentcore-gateway/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Greg Biegel]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 03 Nov 2026 16:47:40 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">1350dc3292e99da0becbb3e875f11092aaa597f1</guid>

					<description>Learn how to configure Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway for an on-behalf-of (OBO) token exchange with Microsoft Entra ID, so an AI agent can call the ServiceNow REST API while preserving the calling user's identity. AgentCore Gateway performs the exchange natively, with no interceptor Lambda or custom code.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;AI agents that interact with enterprise APIs on behalf of users need to maintain user identity throughout the request chain. Without identity propagation, API calls arrive at downstream services as the application itself rather than the user who initiated the action. This breaks audit trails, bypasses user-level access controls, and violates the principle of least privilege.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale, with any framework or model. This post shows how to configure &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, for an on-behalf-of (OBO) token exchange with Microsoft Entra ID. An AI agent can then call the &lt;a href="https://www.servicenow.com/docs/r/api-reference/rest-apis/api-rest.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;ServiceNow REST API&lt;/a&gt; while preserving the calling user’s identity. You configure the token exchange on the gateway target, and AgentCore Gateway performs the exchange on each request. The pattern can be applied to other REST APIs protected by OAuth.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In production, you can additionally pair identity propagation with Amazon Bedrock Guardrails to filter harmful content and validate the agent’s inputs and outputs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The architecture involves three key components connected through AgentCore Gateway:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AI agent application, protected by Microsoft Entra ID through an OAuth application registration.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A Model Context Protocol (MCP) server, implemented using AgentCore Gateway, sitting between the agent and ServiceNow.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The ServiceNow REST API, configured with an inbound OAuth integration tied to Microsoft Entra ID.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following figure shows the end-to-end solution architecture, including components within AWS, Microsoft Entra ID, and ServiceNow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-1.png" alt="Solution architecture showing the agent, AgentCore Gateway, Microsoft Entra ID, and ServiceNow and the token flow between them" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Solution architecture across AWS, Microsoft Entra ID, and ServiceNow&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;As shown in Figure 1, the user acquires a JSON Web Token (JWT) to invoke the agent 1. The agent authenticates to the gateway with a JWT token scoped to the agent’s own application registration 2. The gateway then exchanges that token, using the OBO flow, for a new token scoped to the ServiceNow API connector, with the original user’s identity preserved 3. ServiceNow receives a valid token whose &lt;code&gt;preferred_username&lt;/code&gt; claim maps to an existing ServiceNow user and applies that user’s permissions to the API call 4.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-obo-token-exchange-works-in-this-context"&gt;How OBO token exchange works in this context&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When the agent calls the gateway, it presents a JWT with an &lt;code&gt;audience&lt;/code&gt; claim matching the agent’s client ID (for example, &lt;code&gt;3ff88e17-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXXXXXXXXXX&lt;/code&gt;). ServiceNow, however, expects a token with an &lt;code&gt;audience&lt;/code&gt; claim matching its own API connector client ID (&lt;code&gt;5d4a7a11-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXXXXXXXXXX&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The OBO flow bridges this gap. AgentCore Gateway sends the inbound token to the Entra token endpoint along with the agent application’s client credentials, the target scope, and a parameter &lt;code&gt;requested_token_use: on_behalf_of&lt;/code&gt;. Entra validates that the calling user consented to the delegation, then issues a new token for the ServiceNow API with the original user’s claims intact.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before you begin, you need the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account with access to AgentCore Gateway.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A Microsoft Entra ID tenant with permissions to create &lt;a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/quickstart-register-app" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;application registrations&lt;/a&gt;. Your account needs at least the &lt;a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/role-based-access-control/permissions-reference#application-developer" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Application Developer role&lt;/a&gt; to register applications.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;A ServiceNow instance with administrative access to configure inbound OAuth integrations. You can create a ServiceNow personal developer instance (PDI) at the ServiceNow personal developer instance signup page &lt;a class="uri" href="https://developer.servicenow.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;https://developer.servicenow.com/&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Two Entra application registrations: one representing the AI agent, and one representing the ServiceNow API connector.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="configuring-microsoft-entra-id"&gt;Configuring Microsoft Entra ID&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Start by creating an application registration to represent the ServiceNow API connector. The following screenshot shows the application registration page for the ServiceNow API Connector in Microsoft Entra ID.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-2.png" alt="Microsoft Entra application registration page for the ServiceNow API connector application" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Application registration for the ServiceNow API connector in Microsoft Entra ID&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After creating, in the Overview page note the &lt;strong&gt;Application (client) ID.&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In the navigation menu expand &lt;strong&gt;Manage&lt;/strong&gt; and select &lt;strong&gt;Expose an API&lt;/strong&gt; and add a scope named &lt;code&gt;user_impersonation&lt;/code&gt; using the default Application ID URI. This scope is what the OBO flow requests when exchanging tokens. The following screenshot shows how to expose an API in Microsoft Entra ID.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-3.png" alt="Expose an API page in Microsoft Entra ID with the user_impersonation scope added" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: Exposing the user_impersonation scope in Microsoft Entra ID&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Next, create an application registration for the AI agent. The following screenshot shows the application registration page in Microsoft Entra ID for the ServiceNow Agent application.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-4.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-4.png" alt="Microsoft Entra application registration page for the ServiceNow Agent application" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: Application registration for the ServiceNow Agent in Microsoft Entra ID&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Note the Application (client) ID for the ServiceNowAgent.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Create a client secret on this registration. The gateway needs these credentials to perform the OBO exchange. The following screenshot shows how you can create a client secret on the ServiceNow Agent application registration in Microsoft Entra ID.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-5.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-5.png" alt="Certificates and secrets page showing a new client secret on the ServiceNow Agent registration" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: Creating a client secret on the ServiceNow Agent application registration&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Then configure the agent application to have API permissions to call the ServiceNow API connector application. The following screenshot shows the API permissions that you need to configure on the ServiceNow Agent application registration in Microsoft Entra ID.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-6.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-6.png" alt="API permissions page granting the agent application access to the ServiceNow API connector" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 6: API permission configuration in Microsoft Entra ID&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;No changes to the Entra configuration are needed beyond what you would set up for any standard OBO integration between two applications.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="configuring-servicenow"&gt;Configuring ServiceNow&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In the ServiceNow administrative console, navigate to &lt;strong&gt;System OAuth &amp;gt; Inbound Integrations&lt;/strong&gt; and create a new inbound integration (Third party ID token issued by OIDC supporting identity provider). Specify the client ID from the ServiceNow API connector application registration you created in Entra. The following screenshot shows the configuration of the inbound integration in ServiceNow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-7.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-7.png" alt="ServiceNow inbound integration configured with the API connector client ID" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 7: Configuring the inbound integration in ServiceNow&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In the same screen, create a new OpenID Connect (OIDC) provider configuration and enter the OIDC metadata URL for your Entra tenant. Map the user claim to the appropriate user field. For example, if your Entra tenant places the user’s email in the &lt;code&gt;preferred_username&lt;/code&gt; claim, map that to the &lt;strong&gt;Email&lt;/strong&gt; field in ServiceNow. Clear JTI verification if your Entra tenant doesn’t include it in tokens. Entra uses an internal claim named &lt;code&gt;uti&lt;/code&gt; on tokens it issues, which is equivalent to JTI. Consider mapping this &lt;code&gt;uti&lt;/code&gt; claim to a &lt;code&gt;jti&lt;/code&gt; claim to provide additional replay protection and denylisting. The following screenshot shows how to configure the OIDC provider in ServiceNow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-8.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-8.png" alt="ServiceNow OIDC provider configuration with the Entra metadata URL and claim mapping" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 8: Configuring the OAuth OIDC provider in ServiceNow&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Finally, in the same screen, create an auth scope for the integration (for example, &lt;code&gt;APIConnector&lt;/code&gt;). The following screenshot shows how to create a new auth scope in ServiceNow.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-9.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-9.png" alt="ServiceNow auth scope creation form for the integration" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 9: Creating a new auth scope in ServiceNow&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Limit the auth scope to the Table API, or whichever ServiceNow APIs your agent needs to access, as shown in the following screenshot.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-10.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-10.png" alt="ServiceNow auth scope limited to the Table API" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 10: Limiting the auth scope to the Table API in ServiceNow&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;After this configuration is complete, ServiceNow accepts authorization tokens with an &lt;code&gt;audience&lt;/code&gt; of your API connector’s client ID and resolves the calling user from the &lt;code&gt;preferred_username&lt;/code&gt; claim.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="configuring-agentcore-gateway"&gt;Configuring AgentCore Gateway&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;With Entra and ServiceNow configured, you can now set up AgentCore Gateway to handle the OBO exchange natively.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-1-create-an-outbound-auth-configuration"&gt;Step 1: Create an outbound auth configuration&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Create a new outbound auth configuration (choose &lt;strong&gt;Build &amp;gt; Identity &amp;gt; Add Outbound Auth &amp;gt; Add OAuth client&lt;/strong&gt;) in AgentCore and select &lt;strong&gt;Customized provider&lt;/strong&gt;. Enter the client ID and client secret from your agent application registration, along with the Entra discovery URL as shown in the following screenshot.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-11.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-11.png" alt="AgentCore outbound auth adding an OAuth client with the agent client ID, secret, and Entra discovery URL" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 11: Adding an OAuth client in AgentCore Gateway&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Expand &lt;strong&gt;Additional configurations&lt;/strong&gt; and select &lt;strong&gt;JWT authorization grant&lt;/strong&gt; as shown in the following screenshot. This tells the gateway to use a JWT-based grant type for token exchange.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-12.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-12.png" alt="Additional configurations panel with JWT authorization grant selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 12: Configuring the JWT authorization grant&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-2-create-the-gateway-with-mcp-version-2025-11-25"&gt;Step 2: Create the gateway with MCP version 2025-11-25&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Create a new AgentCore Gateway (&lt;strong&gt;Build &amp;gt; Gateways &amp;gt; Create gateway&lt;/strong&gt;). When defining the gateway details, expand &lt;strong&gt;Additional configurations — optional&lt;/strong&gt; and select MCP version &lt;strong&gt;2025-11-25&lt;/strong&gt; as shown in the following screenshot. This version is required for native OBO support. You can’t turn on native OBO for gateways created with earlier MCP versions.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-13.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-13.png" alt="Gateway creation additional configurations with MCP version 2025-11-25 selected" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 13: Selecting MCP version 2025-11-25&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Configure the inbound identity using your agent’s application registration (select &lt;strong&gt;Use existing identity provider configurations&lt;/strong&gt;), as you would for any gateway that accepts Entra-authenticated requests.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Under &lt;strong&gt;JWT Authorization Configuration&lt;/strong&gt; add the Application ID we documented earlier for the &lt;strong&gt;ServiceNowAgent&lt;/strong&gt; as shown in the following screenshot.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-14.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-14.png" alt="JWT authorization configuration with the ServiceNowAgent application ID added as inbound identity" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 14: Configuring inbound authorization in AgentCore Gateway&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-3-add-a-target-with-token-exchange"&gt;Step 3: Add a target with token exchange&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Add a target to the gateway pointing to your ServiceNow REST endpoint. Define the schema inline. For example, to expose a tool called ListIncidents that calls the ServiceNow Table API, you can use the following OpenAPI specification (replace the server URL with that for your PDI).&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "openapi": "3.0.0",
  "info": {
    "title": "ServiceNow Incident API (Minimal)",
    "description": "A subset of the ServiceNow Table API for listing incident records.",
    "version": "1.0.0"
  },
  "servers": [
    {
      "url": "https://devxxx.service-now.com/api/now/table",
      "variables": {
        "instance": {
          "default": "devxxx",
          "description": "Your ServiceNow instance name."
        }
      }
    }
  ],
  "paths": {
    "/incident": {
      "get": {
        "operationId": "getIncidents",
        "summary": "List incidents",
        "description": "Retrieves a list of incident records from the ServiceNow instance.",
        "parameters": [
          {
            "name": "sysparm_limit",
            "in": "query",
            "description": "Maximum number of records to return.",
            "schema": {
              "type": "integer",
              "default": 10
            }
          },
          {
            "name": "sysparm_query",
            "in": "query",
            "description": "An encoded query string to filter results (e.g., active=true).",
            "schema": {
              "type": "string"
            }
          },
          {
            "name": "sysparm_fields",
            "in": "query",
            "description": "A comma-separated list of fields to return.",
            "schema": {
              "type": "string"
            }
          }
        ],
        "responses": {
          "200": {
            "description": "A successful response containing an array of incidents.",
            "content": {
              "application/json": {
                "schema": {
                  "type": "object",
                  "properties": {
                    "result": {
                      "type": "array",
                      "items": {
                        "$ref": "#/components/schemas/Incident"
                      }
                    }
                  }
                }
              }
            }
          },
          "401": {
            "description": "Unauthorized - Invalid credentials."
          }
        },
        "security": [
          {
            "bearerAuth": []
          }
        ]
      }
    }
  },
  "components": {
    "securitySchemes": {
      "bearerAuth": {
        "type": "http",
        "scheme": "bearer",
        "bearerFormat": "JWT"
      }
    },
    "schemas": {
      "Incident": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
          "sys_id": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "Unique identifier for the incident."
          },
          "number": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "Incident ID (e.g., INC0000001)."
          },
          "short_description": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "A brief summary of the incident."
          },
          "severity": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "The severity level of the incident."
          },
          "state": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "The current state of the incident (e.g., New, In Progress, Closed)."
          },
          "sys_created_on": {
            "type": "string",
            "format": "date-time",
            "description": "The timestamp when the incident was created."
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows how to configure the REST API target in AgentCore Gateway with the OpenAPI schema.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-15.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-15.png" alt="AgentCore Gateway target configured with the ServiceNow OpenAPI schema defined inline" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 15: Configuring the REST API target with the ServiceNow OpenAPI schema&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For the &lt;strong&gt;Outbound Auth configuration&lt;/strong&gt; on the target, select &lt;strong&gt;OAuth&lt;/strong&gt; as the outbound identity provider and choose the outbound auth configuration you created in Step 1.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Now expand the &lt;strong&gt;Additional configurations&lt;/strong&gt; section and select &lt;strong&gt;Token exchange grant&lt;/strong&gt;. Configure the following:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scope:&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;api://5d4a7a11-xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx/user_impersonation&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This is the scope you exposed on the ServiceNow API connector application registration in Entra.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Custom parameter (key-value pair):&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;requested_token_use:on_behalf_of&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This parameter is critical. Without it, Entra doesn’t perform the OBO exchange and the call to ServiceNow fails with an authentication error.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The final configuration is shown in the following screenshot.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-16.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/11/02/ML-21070-16.png" alt="Outbound auth token exchange grant with the user_impersonation scope and requested_token_use custom parameter" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 16: Token exchange grant configuration on the AgentCore Gateway target&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-4-save-and-test"&gt;Step 4: Save and test&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Save the target configuration. AgentCore Gateway now automatically exchanges inbound tokens for valid ServiceNow tokens, preserving user identity, with no custom code.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When an agent calls the gateway, the following happens transparently:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The gateway receives the agent’s JWT (&lt;code&gt;audience&lt;/code&gt;: agent client ID).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The gateway sends the token to Entra’s token endpoint with the OBO grant type.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Entra issues a new token (&lt;code&gt;audience&lt;/code&gt;: ServiceNow API connector client ID) with the original user’s claims.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The gateway forwards the request to ServiceNow with the exchanged token.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;ServiceNow resolves the user from the token and applies their permissions.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following code snippet shows a minimal chat agent that interacts with ServiceNow through the MCP server.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import sys
import strands
from strands.models import BedrockModel
from strands.tools.mcp import MCPClient
from mcp.client.streamable_http import streamablehttp_client

token = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) &amp;gt; 1 else sys.exit("Usage: python snow_agent.py &amp;lt;TOKEN&amp;gt;")
MCP_URL = "&amp;lt;GATEWAY_URL&amp;gt;"
mcp_client = MCPClient(lambda: streamablehttp_client(url=MCP_URL, headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"}))
agent = strands.Agent(
    model=BedrockModel(model_id="global.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6", streaming=False),
    system_prompt=(
        "You have a tool called 'target-snow___getIncidents'. "
        "When asked about incidents, call it immediately with no parameters. "
        "Never ask the user for credentials or instance details --- the tool is pre-configured."
    ),
    tools=[mcp_client],
)

while (user_input := input("\nYou: ")).lower() not in ("quit", "exit", "q"):
    print(f"\nAgent: {agent(user_input)}")&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="troubleshooting"&gt;Troubleshooting&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you receive authentication errors from ServiceNow, verify that:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The &lt;code&gt;preferred_username&lt;/code&gt; claim in the exchanged token maps to a valid user email in ServiceNow.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The auth scope on the ServiceNow inbound integration permits access to the API endpoints your agent is calling.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The agent application has API permissions to the ServiceNow API connector application in Entra.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="cleaning-up"&gt;Cleaning up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, delete the following resources if you created them for testing:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The AgentCore Gateway and its targets.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The outbound auth configuration.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The Entra application registrations (if created specifically for this walkthrough).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;The ServiceNow inbound integration and OIDC provider configuration.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The native OBO support in AgentCore Gateway removes the need for custom AWS Lambda interceptors to perform token exchanges. You configure the token exchange directly on the gateway target, and the gateway handles it transparently on every request. This approach maintains user identity across the agent-to-API chain, supports ServiceNow’s user-level access controls, and eliminates custom code that would need to be maintained separately.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started with AgentCore Gateway, see the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/agentcore.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore documentation&lt;/a&gt;. For more on Microsoft Entra’s OBO flow, refer to the &lt;a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/v2-oauth2-on-behalf-of-flow" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Microsoft identity platform documentation&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt;
